\title{Literature Review Report}
\date{2026-09-03}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
Research question: Is stepwise refinement an established method for developing programs, and did it originate with Wirth's 1971 paper on program development by stepwise refinement? Relevant papers concern top-down program construction, successive refinement of a program from an abstract statement of the task into executable detail, the refinement calculus that formalises it, its use in teaching and in software design methodology, or criticism of it (that real designers work opportunistically rather than top-down, that the documented process is a rational reconstruction, that the method does not scale). Category tags to assign exactly one of: supports-claim = the paper bears on the claim and supports it; qualifies-claim = the paper bears on the claim but contradicts, narrows or complicates it; adjacent-subtopic = same field, different question; off-topic-false-hit = another field, keyword overlap only. The search covered scopus and yielded 1737 papers (593 kept, 1144 discarded, 0 pending).
\end{abstract}
\clearpage
\clearpage
\tableofcontents
\clearpage

\section{Research Context}
Research question: Is stepwise refinement an established method for
developing programs, and did it originate with Wirth's 1971 paper on
program development by stepwise refinement? Relevant papers concern
top-down program construction, successive refinement of a program from
an abstract statement of the task into executable detail, the refinement
calculus that formalises it, its use in teaching and in software design
methodology, or criticism of it (that real designers work
opportunistically rather than top-down, that the documented process is a
rational reconstruction, that the method does not scale). Category tags
to assign exactly one of: supports-claim = the paper bears on the claim
and supports it; qualifies-claim = the paper bears on the claim but
contradicts, narrows or complicates it; adjacent-subtopic = same field,
different question; off-topic-false-hit = another field, keyword overlap
only.
